# Complete JSX Clipscript Production Recipe Playbook (Copy-Ready Prompt Library)

> **The Definitive Engineering Recipe Book for Generative AI Video Prompt Recipes & Formulas**  
> Total Recipes: **336** across **7 Major Sections**  
> Every recipe contains:
> 1. **Copy-Ready Prompt:** Exact prompt format using `(the character from the reference image)`, `(the product from the reference image)`, etc., ready to feed straight into your video generator.
> 2. **Original Example Prompt:** The exact filled prompt used to generate the sample video shown.

## 🎬 Countryside Duo

* **ID:** `genjutsu-27182599-2cee-4b27-be1a-d13e9d713d37` | **Subcategory:** *Curated Multiplier Presets*
* **Visible Effect:** Explore this object replacement preset.
* **Example Clip / Video Preview:** [Watch Video Clip (With Audio)](https://cdn.higgsfield.ai/genjutsu/preview/genjutsu-h264-600-v1/90d01517-46a5-467d-a37e-b2e18b401386.mp4)
* **Example Image Preview:** [View Image](https://cdn.higgsfield.ai/higgsfield_multiplier_video_explore_variant/54b6f52b-08b2-5acf-9557-2e599560acd8.webp)
* **Model Routing:** Keyframe: `Higgsfield Multiplier (hf_mult_replace_object) / Soul 2.0` | Video: `Seedance / Video Diffusion I2V`
* **Format / Ratio:** `16:9 / 9:16` | **Duration:** `14s`

### 1. Copy-Ready Prompt for Your Video Generator
```text
Video-to-video replacement: Maintain original video optical flow, camera perspective, and choreography.

- Replace the main character with (the character from the reference image), locking facial geometry, skin tone, and expressions across all frames.
- Replace the environment with (the background location/setting from the reference image), matching original lighting angle, horizon line, and shadows.
- Preserve temporal stability and motion physics with zero edge flickering or warping.
```

### 2. Original Example Prompt (Generated Video Shown)
```text
Replace the scene location with the environment from my references and change the characters
```

### 3. Step-by-Step Production Pipeline
* **Step 1 (Starting Keyframe):**
  Input reference video: https://d2ol7oe51mr4n9.cloudfront.net/content_user_id/baed0aeb-20d0-54f8-99c5-b4b0cb3a2f66.mp4. Apply replacement prompt: "Replace the scene location with the environment from my references and change the characters".
* **Step 2 (Video Motion Execution):**
  Run motion-conditioned video diffusion to match original optical flow, camera angle, and physics.
* **Negative Prompt (Guardrails):**
  `flickering, lighting mismatch, edge artifacts, inconsistent shadows, temporal warping`

```text
@image3 is the complete appearance reference for the male passenger, including his moustache, tattoos, jewelry, dark shirt, blue-and-lime life vest, blue shorts, and dark water shoes.
@image4 is the mandatory replacement location: a large bright supermarket with polished floors, checkout counters, grocery aisles, carts, shelving, refrigerated cases, high ceilings, white columns, and geometric overhead strip lighting.

SOURCE VIDEO
@video1 is the source video being edited. Preserve the passenger wading up and hopping onto the rear seat behind the woman driver, briefly touching her waist for balance; the driver looking back and then forward while gripping the handlebars; both riders settling into position; the rapid forward acceleration; the passenger holding the driver’s waist; the high-speed forward ride; the rear pursuit view; the lateral high-speed carve; and the final wide crossing movement. Keep both hard cuts and both source spray-like foreground occlusions exactly in place.

SHOT-BY-SHOT GUIDE
Shot 1 (0.0-4.3s): Keep the medium profile tracking shot and pan as the replacement jet ski travels from screen right toward the left foreground. @image1 is seated at the front in the left-center of the frame, facing forward with both hands firmly gripping the black handlebars of @image2. She briefly turns her head over her shoulder toward @image3, then faces forward again. @image3 begins at center-right beside the rear saddle, steps up, swings onto the rear seat behind her, and settles into a forward-facing seated position. He uses one hand on the rear seat and briefly touches @image1’s waist/back to stabilize himself, then holds her waist as the jet ski accelerates. Both riders remain correctly seated on the lime two-level saddle. Restage the source departure across the supermarket’s wide polished floor between the open checkout area and aisles, with the jet ski gliding clear of shelving, carts, checkout counters, and columns. Remove the beach, shallow water, sand, tropical foliage, cliffs, sky, and all outdoor spray. End with the jet ski surging leftward and a bright blurred foreground occlusion matching the source timing.

Shot 2 (4.3-7.3s): Keep the hard cut to the rear tracking chase view. @image1 and @image3 are centered in the midground, riding away from the camera through the open supermarket floor. @image1 sits upright in front, facing away and steering @image2 with both hands on the handlebars. Her long platinum hair trails behind her, and her black glasses stay secure on her face. @image3 sits immediately behind her, leaning slightly forward with both arms around her torso for support and looking ahead past her shoulder. The replacement jet ski travels straight away through the open central aisle, with checkout counters and grocery aisles receding in depth on both sides beneath the geometric white ceiling lights. Keep correct floor contact and scale, with the jet ski passing between visible store fixtures without colliding with them. Remove all ocean water, foam
```

Remove all source paintings, checkered marble, debris, visitors, bronze doors, garden flowers, stone ruins, floating islands, portal, grass, and meadow. Use the ballroom’s polished parquet floor, arched windows, columns, chandeliers, gilded wall details, mirrors, wall lamps, and distant bench as the only environment. Do not add a butterfly or a star, because no butterfly is visible in @video1 and no star reference is supplied. Everything else stays exactly as in @video1: every body movement, pose sequence, running rhythm, handhold, character position, camera move, framing change, hard cut, and timing.

ACTIVE REFERENCES
```text
Use @image1 as the mandatory grand golden ballroom location for the complete video.
Use @image2 for the primary woman’s identity and complete visible wardrobe. Ignore the incidental lavender shoulder bag from @image2.

SOURCE VIDEO
@video1 is the source video being edited. Preserve the primary woman sprinting toward the camera during the rolling shot, slowing near the exit, looking around and briefly glancing back, turning to push open doors, running away from the camera through the next corridor, and reaching the second woman for a joyful two-handed spin. Preserve all three hard cuts, the full barrel-roll camera movement in the opening sprint, the forward tracking shots, and the final upward crane pullback.

SHOT-BY-SHOT GUIDE
Shot 1 (0-6.8s): Keep the frontal medium-wide tracking shot that rapidly pulls backward and performs the full 360-degree barrel roll. @image2 runs directly toward the camera in the center foreground, front-facing with high knee drive, steady arm pumps, and a focused determined gaze ahead. Her feet land naturally on the ballroom’s polished parquet floor. The ballroom’s tall arched windows, columns, chandeliers, gilded walls, and warm reflections rotate with the source camera move. Remove the collapsing museum walls, framed paintings, checkered floor, visitors, and all flying debris. She slows near the far end of the ballroom before the cut.

Shot 2 (6.8-10.6s): Keep the eye-level medium shot and slight forward camera track. @image2 stands centered at the far end of the ballroom, looks around, briefly glances back toward the camera, turns 180 degrees, then faces the tall white paneled glass doors at the right end of @image1’s ballroom. She raises both arms and pushes the doors outward with both palms at chest height, keeping the same forceful push and timing. Bright daylight floods through the opened doors. Shot 3 (10.6-18.5s): Keep the medium-wide third-person following shot tracking forward behind @image2. She runs away from the camera along the center of the polished ballroom floor, her blonde hair bouncing with the same cadence. She passes beneath the ballroom’s repeated arched window bays and between the visible columns toward the bright open doors in the distance. Her feet remain grounded on the parquet floor. Remove the stone path, glowing flowers, floating islands, ruins, grass, and stone archway; do not recreate a portal. She reaches the daylight-filled doorway at the end of the shot.

Shot 4 (18.5-23.3s): Keep the medium two-shot that rises smoothly into an extreme high-angle wide crane shot pulling away overhead. In the open central floor area of the ballroom, @image2 stands center-left facing the second woman at center-right. They clasp both hands at waist height, look directly into each other’s faces, smile broadly, lean back slightly, and rotate together in the same joyful circle. Their feet stay planted and step naturally across the parquet floor as the second woman’s white tiered dress flares. The rising view reveals the ballroom’s windows, chandeliers, gilded walls, columns, and floor depth at correct scale. Remove the flower field, grass, blossoms, and horizon haze.
```

### 2. Original Example Prompt (Generated Video Shown)
```text
SCENE CONTEXT  
A one-take beverage spot: exactly one single continuous 10-second shot in 16:9, filmed by one camera in one unbroken take. There is no editing in this video — no cuts, no dissolves, no transitions, no shot changes of any kind. A bright sunny day in a colorful old-town street alive with people: the camera descends from directly above the street to a young man crouched at its center holding an orange-pink can of FLAVOURED DRINK, then circles him once as he toasts the lens.

ACTIVE REFERENCES  
<<<image_1>>> is the product reference: a standard aluminum can with a vibrant orange-to-pink gradient body, a holographic silver wave across the lower half, white bubble-outline lettering reading FLAVOURED DRINK with the word original beneath it, silver top, fine condensation droplets. The can design, gradient, wave and all label text match the reference exactly and stay legible; the label generally faces the camera.  
<<<image_2>>> is the character reference: a young man with messy light-brown hair and freckles, a grey backwards cap, silver hoop earring, thin chain necklace, salmon-red boxy t-shirt over a cream long-sleeve, olive cargo pants, black tote bag across his chest, dark loafers. Face, build, outfit and identity 100% match the reference throughout — he is the only person in sharp focus.  
<<<image_3>>> is the location reference: a colorful old-town pedestrian street — yellow, red, turquoise and white facades with painted signs, string lights overhead, café tables, potted plants, a leafy tree, sandy ground, warm daylight. Inherit the geometry, palette and light direction; do not alter the layout. Background signage stays as in the reference, soft and unreadable — no new text anywhere.

SPATIAL MAP  
The street runs between the yellow and red facades. He crouches on its center axis. A lively crowd fills the edges — people chatting at café tables, strollers along the facades, a cyclist far in the background — leaving the center lane around him open. String lights cross overhead between the rooflines.

FIRST FRAME  
First frame: the camera is INSIDE the street canyon, hovering just above the string lights, looking steeply down — both colorful facades framing the edges, the crouched character visible small at the center below, the crowd dotting the edges. The facades remain visible in frame from this very first frame until the end of the shot, proving the space is continuous. No sky-only frames, no fade-in, no logo cards, no titles.

CAMERA  
The entire video is one uninterrupted camera movement, flowing like a single drone take: from its start position just above the string lights the camera descends smoothly and steadily straight down toward him, the facades sliding upward past the frame edges, the crowd growing life-size, and — without ever stopping or jumping — the descent bends into a forward glide that levels out at his eye height about three meters in front of him at around the 3-second mark; from there, in the same continuous motion, the camera curves sideways into a smooth circle around him at constant height, constant distance and constant speed, completing one full 360-degree circle by the 10-second mark, the facades, string lights, café tables, tree and softly blurred crowd wheeling past behind him with natural parallax. He stays in frame continuously from the first frame to the last — the camera never loses sight of him, which is what makes this a single take. As the circle crosses his front around the 6-second mark, he raises the can toward the lens, label square to camera, held long enough to read FLAVOURED DRINK clean. There are no other camera movements, no zooms, no speed ramps, and at no point does the image cut, blink, dissolve or reframe.

ACTION  
He is crouched easily at the street's center, forearms on knees, the orange-pink can loose in one hand, label outward. As the camera levels in front of him he lifts his eyes to the lens with an unhurried grin. When the circling camera crosses his front he raises the can in a small confident toast — condensation catching the sun — lowers it, and holds his relaxed pose as the circle completes. Around him the crowd lives naturally: café laughter, a couple strolling in the blurred background, someone glancing over with a smile at his toast. Final frame: three-quarter view of him grinning at the lens, can on his knee, label visible, the colorful street and its people glowing softly behind him.

PHYSICS  
Real body weight: knees loaded in the crouch, a subtle balance shift on the toast, the can solid in one hand — never switching hands, clipping or floating; one condensation drop slides on the raise. The crowd moves with natural pedestrian rhythm, each person staying in their zone at the edges, no one crossing between the camera and him. The camera's descent, glide and circle are one continuous flow of momentum — decelerating and curving like a real drone, never teleporting, never resetting position. Real-time speed throughout, no slow motion anywhere.

LIGHTING  
Bright warm midday sun: golden light on the facades, crisp soft-edged shadows from the tree and string lights on the sandy ground, the can's orange-pink gradient glowing saturated with the holographic wave throwing subtle rainbow speculars and a sun-flare kiss on the toast, his face lit by warm bounce off the walls — sunny, saturated, appetizing light that stays consistent through the whole take.

AUDIO  
One continuous soundscape with no audio cuts: soft wind settling as the camera descends, birdsong and lively café chatter rising, an upbeat feel-good funk-pop track dropping its first beat exactly as the camera levels in front of him, grooving through the circle, a crisp aluminum click and fizz-hiss on the toast, a warm ripple of nearby laughter, the track resolving on the final frame. No dialogue, no voiceover.

STYLE  
Photorealistic premium one-take beverage-spot cinematography, in the tradition of single-shot drone commercials: crisp digital cinema image, saturated sunny palette harmonizing with the can's orange-pink gradient, crowd in soft bokeh, confident continuous drone energy, every frame poster-clean.

QUALITY  
Stable rendering at constant real speed: he stays on-model from every angle of the circle; the can stays exact — gradient, wave and label sharp and unwarped whenever it faces camera; the crowd stays smooth in the blur with no flicker, popping or duplicated people; no ghosting, no duplicated limbs, no facade warping during the descent.

POSITIVE LOCKS  
This video is one single continuous take: one camera, one unbroken movement, zero cuts, zero dissolves, zero transitions, zero shot changes — the character remains continuously visible in frame from the first frame to the last, and the environment transforms only by parallax as the camera moves. He matches <<<image_2>>> exactly from every angle and is the only person in sharp focus; the crowd stays at the edges, softly blurred, never crossing between camera and character. The street matches <<<image_3>>> throughout; the can matches <<<image_1>>> exactly, its label text — FLAVOURED DRINK, original — always accurate, never garbled. No new text, labels, watermarks or UI beyond the can's own label. Real-time speed throughout, no slow motion.

OUTPUT SETTINGS  
Landscape 16:9, 10 seconds, real-time motion speed, native audio generation.
```

### 1. Copy-Ready Prompt for Your Video Generator
```text
Video-to-video replacement: Maintain original video optical flow, camera perspective, and choreography.

- Replace the main character with (the character from the reference image), locking facial geometry, skin tone, and expressions across all frames.
- Replace the environment with (the background location/setting from the reference image), matching original lighting angle, horizon line, and shadows.
- Preserve temporal stability and motion physics with zero edge flickering or warping.
```

### 2. Original Example Prompt (Generated Video Shown)
```text
SCENE CONTEXT  
One continuous 10-second infinity zoom-out shot for a bright, hyper-glossy Y2K music video, in which the entire visible world spins slowly and continuously clockwise inside the frame from the very first frame to the very last, completing exactly one full revolution. The camera never cuts. It pulls backward the whole time while the world turns: horizons tilt, walls rotate past, floors climb the frame edges. Each scene is revealed to be a live image nested inside an object in the next scene. Every nested hand-off is purely geometric — the ongoing scene stays fully solid and opaque while its container simply grows in around it as the camera pulls back; nothing ever fades, dissolves, or turns transparent. The final scene is the same city street as the opening, arriving right-side-up again as the revolution completes, closing a perfect loop.

ACTIVE REFERENCES  
<<<image_1>>> is the STREET MAN character reference — Y2K man: yellow striped beanie, yellow open-mesh tee over white long-sleeve, chunky silver chains with a square pendant, white bead bracelet, black jorts with a huge silver ball chain, yellow-black-white sneakers. Identical to this reference wherever he appears.  
<<<image_2>>> is the BATH GIRL character reference — Y2K girl: long black hair with wispy bangs, hot-pink halter crop top with a white satin bow, silver star belly chain, star necklace, pink bead bracelet. Identical to this reference (in the tub, visible waist-up above the foam).  
<<<image_3>>> is the ARCADE BOY character reference — Y2K boy: backwards royal-blue cap over dark curls, thin mustache, blue mesh V-neck jersey over white tee, shell necklace, stud earrings, baggy washed denim, blue-white sneakers. Identical to this reference.  
<<<image_4>>> is the BARBER GIRL character reference — Y2K girl: long braids with lime-green strands, lime-green off-shoulder long-sleeve crop top, gold bangles and earrings, gold plate necklace, lime belt with a jeweled buckle and a lime bandana at her hip, grey baggy pants, lime fuzzy leg warmers, green-and-white nails. Identical to this reference.  
<<<image_5>>> is the STREET location reference — sunlit London-style street: red, yellow and teal shopfronts, brick uppers, blue sky, a red barbershop storefront with a chrome barber pole at frame left. Controls scene 1 and scene 5. All existing signage stays exactly as in the reference; no new readable text ever appears anywhere in the video.

TEXT-DEFINED LOCATIONS (no image references — build these from description, in the same bright Y2K world)  
BATHROOM (scene 2): a vivid hot-pink glossy-tiled bathroom with turquoise trim, a white bathtub crowned with dense turquoise foam, gold vintage fixtures, colorful bottles on a pink shelf, and a gold-framed picture hanging on the tiled wall directly above the tub — the frame where the live street scene lives.  
ARCADE (scene 3): a dark neon arcade with rows of decorated cabinets, curved glowing CRT screens, red joysticks and colorful buttons, pink and green neon tubes glowing against the dark — the foreground cabinet's CRT is where the live bathroom scene plays.  
BARBERSHOP (scene 4): a bright modern barbershop with a black-and-white checkerboard floor, red leather barber chairs, bulb-lit mirrors, shelves of colorful product bottles, a chrome-and-glass front looking onto the street, a spinning barber pole visible outside, and a slim wall-mounted flatscreen TV high on the wall — the TV where the live arcade scene plays.

FIRST FRAME AND SPATIAL BLOCKING  
First frame, right-side-up: the man from <<<image_1>>> stands center frame on the sunlit street from <<<image_5>>>, facing camera, mid-gesture with one hand raised, framed waist-up, colorful shopfronts running deep behind him. In the very first second the whole street already begins to tilt clockwise in frame — the horizon visibly rotating, buildings starting to lean. This exact live image is what later fills the gold frame in the bathroom.

FORMAT MODE  
16:9 horizontal, single continuous take, 10 seconds.

OPTICS  
84° diagonal field of view, classic wide rectilinear character. Lens locked for the whole shot; all zoom-out is physical camera pull-back, no focal-length drift, no fisheye.

CAMERA  
The camera does two things at once for all 10 seconds without ever stopping: it pulls backward continuously, and it rolls clockwise on its optical axis in one slow unbroken spin, so the world inside the frame is always visibly turning — never level, never holding, never counter-rotating. The rotation is slow enough that faces stay readable but always clearly in motion. Orientation checkpoints: at 2.5s the world lies fully sideways (90°), at 5s fully upside-down (180°), at 7.5s sideways the other way (270°), and only in the final frame at 10s does the world return exactly right-side-up. The spin carries seamlessly across every scene transition with no reset and no pause.

ACTION TIMING  
0.0–1.5s — STREET: the man from <<<image_1>>> bounces on the beat with one big clear arm swing while the street from <<<image_5>>> tilts clockwise past 30-40°; the camera pulls straight back and, at full solidity, gold frame edges grow in from outside the frame around the still-live street image — the street stays fully opaque and vivid inside the frame, never fading.  
1.5–2.2s — TRANSITION 1: the gold-framed picture — its live street image solid, opaque and unchanged — shrinks on the pink tiled wall above the tub as the camera pulls back, revealing the bathroom around it while the whole tiled wall keeps rotating in frame. The framed image never becomes transparent and never blends with anything behind it.  
2.2–3.6s — BATHROOM: the girl from <<<image_2>>> sits in the tub crowned with turquoise foam, throwing one big two-finger gesture at camera; the world is near 90°, tile grout lines running vertically, everything still turning as the camera pulls back; in the final moments faint scanlines and a soft phosphor bloom appear ON TOP of the fully solid image — an overlay on a 100% opaque picture, not a fade.  
3.6–4.5s — TRANSITION 2 (CRT reveal): a thick dark bezel creeps in from all four frame edges around the still fully opaque, fully vivid bathroom image, revealing it glowing on the curved CRT of the foreground arcade cabinet in extreme close-up; the camera keeps pulling back and the live, solid screen image shrinks with its bezel into the decorated cabinet body, joystick and buttons appearing below, neon pink and green light resolving around it. The bathroom stays live and opaque on the screen the entire time — no transparency, no ghosting, no dissolve.  
4.5–5.8s — ARCADE: the boy from <<<image_3>>> stands at the cabinet, one hand on the joystick, then pushes off and nods hard, arms spreading wide, neon washing over him; at 5s the arcade hangs fully upside-down — cabinets pointing down from the top of frame, neon inverted — and the spin continues through it without pausing.  
5.8–6.4s — TRANSITION 3: a slim black TV bezel grows in around the still fully opaque upside-down arcade image, revealing it playing on the wall-mounted flatscreen TV high on the barbershop wall; the solid screen image shrinks with its bezel as the room keeps turning. No fade, no transparency at any point.  
6.4–7.9s — BARBERSHOP: the girl from <<<image_4>>> sits in the red leather barber chair in a black cape open over her lime outfit, chin up, nodding on the beat and brushing a hand across her jawline, gold bangles catching the bulb light; the world is near 270°, the checkerboard floor climbing the left side of frame, bulb-lit mirrors sideways, all still turning clockwise.  
7.9–8.6s — TRANSITION 4: the camera pulls backward through the barbershop's rotating glass front — the interior staying fully solid and visible through the transparent glass, exposure shifting naturally from bulbs to daylight — revealing the shop as the red storefront with the chrome barber pole on the sunlit street from <<<image_5>>>. The only transparency in this transition is the real glass of the window itself; the scene never dissolves.  
8.6–10.0s — STREET AGAIN: the camera keeps pulling back along the same sunlit street, the barbershop storefront at frame left, the man from <<<image_1>>> visible again center frame in the distance mid-bounce; the street rotates through the final degrees and settles exactly right-side-up on the last frame, recreating the opening composition.

PHYSICS  
Gravity stays true to each world even as the frame rotates: foam clings and slides downward on the shoulders of the girl from <<<image_2>>>, the cape drapes naturally on the girl from <<<image_4>>>, the barber pole spins on its own axis — only the camera's orientation turns, never the physics. Every nested image is a fully opaque physical surface at 100% visibility from the moment it appears to the moment it leaves frame: the framed print is solid paper with slight glare, the CRT is solid glowing curved glass with scanlines, the TV is a solid flat panel with faint glass glare — none are ever translucent, none ever show anything through themselves, and no two scenes are ever visible blended in the same pixels. Each nested image stays anchored inside its frame or bezel as it shrinks — it does not slide, peel, detach, fade, or dissolve. The storefront glass carries a brief real reflection of the street as the camera passes through the window plane. Each nested surface keeps correct perspective as the camera recedes and rotates.

LIGHTING  
Street: clean high midday sun, crisp shadows, saturated color. Bathroom: bright bounced light off pink tiles, glossy highlights on foam and skin, gold fixtures glinting. Arcade: saturated neon pink and green practicals, CRT glow spilling onto the jersey of the boy from <<<image_3>>>, face readable. Barbershop: warm bulb-light from the mirror frames, clean bright key, color pops from bottle shelves and red chairs. The barbershop-to-street transition rides a natural interior-to-daylight exposure shift.

AUDIO  
No music, no dialogue, no voices — silent visual designed for a track to be laid over in the edit.

STYLE  
Bright, hyper-glossy Y2K music-video aesthetic: bubblegum pinks, lime greens, chrome and silver jewelry, mesh jerseys, baby tees, bead accessories, baggy denim, glossy plastic sheen, punchy saturated digital cinematography with a subtle early-2000s glamour bloom — playful, flirty, maximalist.

QUALITY  
Faces, gestures and nested objects stay sharp and readable throughout the continuous spin, with natural subtle motion blur from the rotation and pull-back. Every nested image remains at full opacity and full saturation through its entire transition — no cross-dissolves, no double exposures, no ghost overlaps between scenes, no translucent frames or screens. The CRT reveal stays crisp: bezel edges clean, screen image unbroken and solid. No ghosting, no flicker beyond the intentional CRT scanline quality, no duplicated limbs, no frame-skipping stutter, no cuts, and the rotation never freezes, never snaps level, and never reverses.

POSITIVE CONSTRAINTS  
Exactly one person is visible per scene, each matching their reference exactly: <<<image_1>>> in scenes 1 and 5, <<<image_2>>> in scene 2, <<<image_3>>> in scene 3, <<<image_4>>> in scene 4. The street matches <<<image_5>>> in scenes 1 and 5 with its signage unchanged; the bathroom, arcade and barbershop follow their text descriptions in the same bright Y2K world, and no readable text appears anywhere in any scene. The camera only ever moves backward and the world in frame is always visibly rotating clockwise — sideways at 2.5s, upside-down at 5s, sideways again at 7.5s, right-side-up only on the final frame at 10s. All four scene hand-offs are purely geometric nestings performed at full opacity: gold-framed picture, arcade CRT screen, flatscreen TV, barbershop storefront window — the inner scene stays 100% solid, vivid and live while its container grows in around it, and at no point does any image fade, dissolve, turn transparent, or blend with another scene. The final frame matches the opening street composition, completing the loop.
```

### 1. Copy-Ready Prompt for Your Video Generator

```text
Video-to-video replacement: Maintain original video optical flow, camera perspective, and choreography.

- Replace the main character with (the character from the reference image), locking facial geometry, skin tone, and expressions across all frames.
```

### 2. Original Example Prompt (Generated Video Shown)
```text
SCENE CONTEXT  
A one-take ride shot: exactly one single continuous 8-second shot in 16:9, filmed by one flying camera that is PHYSICALLY MOVING THROUGH THE AIR during every single second of the video — it never stands still, never hovers in place, never becomes a static tripod shot. There is no editing — no cuts, no dissolves, no transitions, no shot changes of any kind. A coaster car full of five euphoric friends races through the drop and bends of a red roller coaster in a sunlit vintage fairground, while the flying camera travels one big smooth circle AROUND the ride, keeping the car in frame every moment from first frame to last.

ACTIVE REFERENCES  
<<<image_1>>> is the location reference: a vintage seaside fairground under deep blue sky — a red-and-white roller coaster with sweeping bends on the left, a large white Ferris wheel, a gilded carousel with white horses, red-and-white striped stalls and tents, strings of red and yellow bunting, balloons, a cotton-candy cart with a pink fringed umbrella. Controls the environment, rides, palette and light; existing stall signage stays exactly as in the reference, soft and unreadable — no new readable text appears anywhere.

THE RIDERS (text-defined, consistent for the whole shot)  
Five friends in their twenties packed into one coaster car, two rows: front row — a laughing young woman with flying auburn hair and sunglasses in a navy jacket, and a dark-haired young woman in a knit sweater; back row — a tall young man in a dark suit jacket with both arms raised high, a blond young man mid-scream, and a red-haired young woman laughing with her head tipped back. All casual-smart, all radiating uncontainable joy.

FIRST FRAME AND SPATIAL BLOCKING  
First frame: the camera is already flying sideways through the air — the fairground visibly sliding across the frame with parallax from the very first frame — with the red coaster structure at center, the packed car just tipping over its first crest, five riders clearly visible; the Ferris wheel, carousel, striped stalls and bunting surround the coaster on all sides.

OPTICS  
35° natural wide cinematic rendering, crisp and rectilinear, at a fixed focal length — no zooming in or out; every change of framing comes from the camera physically flying closer, farther, or around.

CAMERA — CONSTANT FLIGHT  
For all 8 seconds the camera is an FPV drone in continuous flight along one unbroken counter-clockwise circular path around the roller coaster structure, at rider height, moving fast enough that the background NEVER stops sliding: in every single frame of the video, the Ferris wheel, carousel, stalls, bunting and sky are visibly traveling across the frame with strong parallax — this continuous background motion is the proof that the camera is always flying. The circle's radius breathes as it flies — sweeping closer as the car drops toward the camera's side of the structure, arcing wider as the car banks away — but the flight never stops, never pauses, never reverses, never hovers, and never jumps. Because the camera keeps traveling around the ride while the car races through drops and bends inside the circle, their relative angle changes constantly: the riders are seen head-on, then in profile, then from behind, then in profile again, all within the one unbroken lap. The coaster car remains in frame for every single frame. No cuts, no reframes, no teleports, no speed ramps, no static moments.

ACTION TIMING  
0-1.5s: the car tips over the crest and begins to fall — hair floating, the suited man's arms stretching high — while the flying camera sweeps along its arc toward the drop's side, the fairground streaming across the background behind the riders.  
1.5-3.5s: THE DROP — the car plunges down the big descent, hair and jackets whipping violently upward, all five screaming with laughter; the camera's flight carries it past the falling car in the opposite direction, the crossing speeds making the plunge feel ferocious, the background whipping past.  
3.5-5.5s: the car hits the bottom and slings through its hard banked turn, the five friends leaning in unison, the front woman throwing a fist up; the still-flying camera catches them in profile as it continues its lap, the carousel and striped stalls streaking behind them.  
5.5-8s: the car swoops through the far bend and rises onto the next straight as the camera's lap curves around to meet it nearly head-on — the camera still traveling laterally as the car races toward it — the riders' breathless laughing faces growing larger, hair settling, the dark-haired woman's arms up now too. Final frame: the car racing toward the moving camera, five beaming faces readable, the Ferris wheel still sliding across the sky behind them, sun flaring at the frame edge.

PHYSICS

Constant real-time speed throughout, no slow motion anywhere.

Bright golden afternoon sun in a deep blue sky with scattered white clouds: the sun travels around the frame as the camera flies its lap — flaring into the lens on one side of the circle, backlighting flying hair on the opposite side — faces lit by open-sky bounce, the fairground saturated and sunlit in every direction. Natural lens flares from the real sun only.

Roaring wind rush that never dies down — the constant airflow of a camera in flight; the rhythmic clatter of coaster wheels swelling and softening as the flying camera nears and recedes from the track; five voices screaming, whooping and laughing in real bursts; the fairground rotating through the soundfield — carousel organ, crowd murmur, a ride bell — panning continuously with the camera's lap; a shared burst of breathless laughter as the car races toward the lens at the end. No dialogue lines, no narration, no music track.
```

LIGHTING

```text
- Hair, beard, tattoos and accessories carry over. Clothing from your photos is ignored, so the hero fights shirtless in boxing gear.
```

### 2. Original Example Prompt (Generated Video Shown)
```text
Recreate this as a 21-second, 30-shot paper-collage music video starring the user's own people, cut on the beat (129 BPM).

Keep every shot's camera move, timing, framing and collage trick: magazine cut-outs rebuilding a face, stickers on a face, recursive zoom through TV screens, face slices, kaleidoscope, mirrored profiles, torn paper wall, paper mask ripped off, ruffled collar in clouds, and the finale where the face assembles from porcelain shards. Only the people and the art change.

People must stay photoreal live action with their exact faces, hair and outfits from the user's photos. They must not look like an illustration, cartoon or restyle. With fewer photos than roles, the same people repeat across shots. Use new bright, vivid editorial backgrounds and papers in every shot.

All shots are 16:9 with heads and feet fully in frame. Footage plays in real time and is never sped up. Hard cuts on the beat, with punch-ins, white flashes and RGB-split hits. Keep the preset's soundtrack unless the user gives their own track.

No on-screen text, captions, logos or watermarks. No black or empty frames, no repeated shots.
```

### 2. Original Example Prompt (Generated Video Shown)
```text
start a FREQUENCY video
Make me the FREQUENCY video with my character as the hero.

MY CHARACTER
- Images: [attached: 5-20 images of the same character (face front, 3/4, profile, full body, same outfit) or one character sheet]
- Name for the files: [e.g. Mira]
- Signature look in one line: [e.g. sharp platinum bob, round gold glasses, long white trench coat, white boots]
- This is: [me / a fictional character / a person who agreed to it]

WHAT I WANT
- Variants: [both / branded only / clean only]   (branded = Higgsfield lettering and end card, clean = no lettering at all)
- Music: [attached track I have the rights to / silent]
- Finale white eyes: [yes / no]
- Anything to keep or avoid: [e.g. keep my tattoos visible, never show me smoking / nothing]

HOW TO MAKE IT
1. Unzip `frequency_template.zip`. If no FREQUENCY Director system prompt is loaded, read `SYSTEM_PROMPT.md` and
   `README.md` from the bundle first and follow them as your instructions.
2. Keep the FREQUENCY edit exactly: 571 frames at 30 fps, every cut, effect and transition, 16:9 plus 9:16.
3. Stills only in Soul Cinema with a trained Soul character of me; motion only in Seedance 2.5 with my character sheet
   as reference. All effects and transitions in the template compositor.
4. I am the only person in the video. Where a shot needs several people, use copies of me. No other humans.
5. I must be clearly recognisable in every shot, my signature look visible even in silhouettes.
6. No text, logos or watermarks inside the generated images.
7. Tell me the credit estimate before the big batches, then go ahead.
8. Show me a 3-shot identity test before the full batch. After I approve it, go to the end without stopping.

DELIVER
For each variant: 16:9 1080p, 9:16 1080x1920 and 16:9 without audio. Then tell me which shots stayed stills, if any.
```

### 2. Original Example Prompt (Generated Video Shown)
```text
Edit the supplied video by replacing its main performer with the character shown in <<<image_1>>>. Apply the replacement throughout the entire clip, including every repeated or duplicated appearance of that performer. Keep unrelated people unchanged.

REFERENCE PRIORITY
Use the video for movement, performance timing, expressions, interactions and camera behavior.
Use <<<image_1>>> for the replacement character’s identity, physique, body proportions, hairstyle, clothing and accessories. If <<<image_1>>> includes multiple views, treat them as views of one character: facial close-ups define identity, and full-body views define anatomy, proportions and wardrobe.

COMPLETE CHARACTER REPLACEMENT
Replace the entire visible person, not just the face. Preserve the character’s build, shoulder width, torso shape, limb proportions and head-to-body ratio as shown in <<<image_1>>>.

Retarget the source performance to that anatomy. Preserve the original action sequence, rhythm, movement through the scene and grounded foot contacts, adapting the poses naturally to the replacement body. Do not inherit the original performer’s physique or force the new character into the original silhouette. Clothing should fit and move with the body shown in <<<image_1>>>.

Preserve the character’s recognizable appearance through turns, expressions, partial occlusion and motion blur. Transfer the performance’s expression changes without copying the original face or freezing the expression from <<<image_1>>>. Keep clothing and accessories consistent throughout.

EXISTING EDIT
Preserve the source duration, aspect ratio, framing, camera movement, cuts, transitions, playback speed, audio and visual effects. Do not invent new choreography or camera moves.

Replace the performer in close-ups, wide shots, isolated body details, reflections and any existing duplicates or motion trails. Preserve the timing, placement and transparency of those effects, with every appearance depicting the same character from <<<image_1>>>. Remove remnants of the original performer’s face, body and clothing.

SCENE INTEGRATION
Match each shot’s lighting, perspective, focus, grain and motion blur. Recalculate shadows, occlusion and clothing movement for the replacement silhouette. Preserve believable contact with the ground, props and other people.

Keep the original environment, objects, overlays and shots without the target performer unchanged. Reconstruct background areas exposed by the new silhouette consistently with the surrounding scene.

The replacement is present from the first applicable frame. No transformation sequence, identity flicker, body-shape drift, extra limbs or added characters. Do not import the background, layout or reference-sheet elements from <<<image_1>>>.

CHARACTER LOCK (from <<<image_1>>>, zone by zone)
- Face: <age, gender, skin tone, features, eye colour, makeup, lips, facial hair>. <Piercings and glasses shown, or none>.
- Hair: <length, colour, texture, parting>.
- Ears: <earrings, or none>.
- Neck: <what is shown, or bare skin>. Nothing else around the neck.
- Torso: <each layer: garment, colour, material, fit, how it is worn>.
- Hands: <wrists>; <nails: length, shape, colour>; <rings, or none>.
- Legs and feet: <bottoms>, <shoes>.

SOURCE CLEAN-UP
<Performer to replace: who, only if several people.> The original performer has <hair, garments with prints, neckband, piercings, tattoos, nails, wrist items, glasses: only what differs from the character>. Carry none of them over, not even in a changed form: <e.g. the neckband does not become a choker; long nails do not stay long; the wrist band does not become a bracelet>. This holds in every shot, including <close-ups, colored-light shots, hand details, clones>.
```

```text
- Execute the sequence: Recreate this exact edit with the user's character as the only rider. Keep the reference's structure 1:1: 27.5 s, 16:9, 24 fps, the same shot order and cut map, hard cuts locked to the reference music (the main hit at 15.3 s after a short bass drop-o...
```

### 2. Original Example Prompt (Generated Video Shown)
```text
Recreate this exact edit with the user's character as the only rider. Keep the reference's structure 1:1: 27.5 s, 16:9, 24 fps, the same shot order and cut map, hard cuts locked to the reference music (the main hit at 15.3 s after a short bass drop-out), 1–3-frame flash inserts, and the reference's original soundtrack. No generated audio, no new music.

LOOK: high-contrast black-and-white, deep blacks, hard white sun on snow, snow dust in the air, film grain, light vignette. The only colour is small red-orange accents: a red work lamp at night, red light leaks, a red duotone on a few action frames, a pink-magenta duotone on the cliff-drop silhouette. Keep the reference's graphic layer minimal and exact: thin white frame lines and small rectangles, halftone and invert flashes, smear cuts, one short glitch block. Never text.

CHARACTER: first build the user's character (character sheet, hero portrait, helmet-and-goggles look) with Seedream 5 Pro and keep the identical face in every shot. Wardrobe: black unbranded freeride shell jacket and bib pants, black gloves and boots, matte black helmet, mirror goggles, a plain matte-black snowboard. One rider per shot, no other people. Locations with Soul Cinema: powder face, high peak, cornice ridge, rock band, night slope under one work lamp, sun haze.

POSING BEATS (the heart of the edit): the character presents themselves to the camera at three moments:
- the hook (0–3 s): a night close-up in helmet and goggles lit by the red lamp, then goggles up and a direct stare;
- around the drop (15.3 s): takes the helmet off for a bare-headed stare, then stands tall on the ridge;
- the ending: walks toward the lens with the board on the shoulder, and the final close-up portrait holds to the last frame.
Serious and confident, at most a faint smile in the very last close-up.

ACTION: high-mountain freeride. Toeside carves throwing big powder fans, a top-down aerial of the rider on a steep face, a cliff drop silhouetted against the sky, a cornice-ridge run, boot and binding close-ups, a behind-the-shoulder drop-in, a night run under the lamp. Every shot is an animated Seedance 2.5 clip with real physical motion and natural motion blur: no still images, no dead holds, no repeated shots, no CG morphs.

NEVER: titles, captions, logos, brand marks, numbers, end cards or watermarks; merged or duplicated bodies, two boards, extra limbs, melting faces, identity drift.
```

```text
Remix this edit for the user's character: keep its style and editing, invent new content around the inputs; do not copy it shot for shot. KEEP: 13.0 s, 1920×1080, 30 fps, the edit's own soundtrack at 132 BPM, cuts and graphic hits on beats and half-beats, act changes at 0.3, 1.43, 2.2, 3.93, 5.73, 9.37, 11.2 and 12.1 s. Look: high-key hero plates on a seamless white cyclorama, matted onto hand-made zine pages (paper #FAF9F6, ink #0B0B0B, orange #F26A1B, cyan #2BB6F0, kraft #D6893E, starburst #EC601C, magenta #E23AD8, red cutout outline #E8231C), light grain. Arc: 0–0.3 brush-letter title; 0.3–1.43 hidden hero, head bowed so his silhouette reads as an emblem, slowly rising; 1.43–2.2 his signature micro-gesture inside a framed photo panel; 2.2–3.93 a graph-paper spec sheet of him with leader labels; 3.93–5.73 he sips a drink he never spills, then stands still in a gale; 5.73–9.37 a squad of 4–6 black-clad masked ninjas attacks and he beats them with minimal effort, drink still in hand (two collide mid-air, a wrist-catch flip, a lazy kick), ending on the fallen ninjas with a ONE/TWO/THREE count; 9.37–11.2 a quiet white pocket that freezes on his stare; 11.2–12.1 final stare; 12.1–13.0 black end card. The ninja fight is mandatory for every character: never drop or replace the ninjas. Grammar: 1–2-frame swaps between colour, a xerox-ink threshold and a hand-drawn comic redraw of the same frame; stepped 12 fps plates; a grey halftone echo of the hero at 1.9× behind him; black brush swooshes; single-frame grey, black and white solids; invert flashes and RGB-split ramps into pixel glitch; displaced frame slices with flat RGB slabs kept off the face; radial or linear speed lines; a 1-frame impact frame (black ground, white silhouettes) followed by a 4-frame shake; punch-in zooms and slow 5 % push-ins; a freeze with a red offset outline. Type: brush-marker capitals for title and end card; bold mono capitals typed at 0.04 s per letter for headers and leader labels; one-word comic stickers on rounded plaques (orange #F27828 or yellow #F5C832, 7 px black outline, hard shadow) that pop over 4 frames in corners, never on the face. ADAPT: the gesture, the kind of drink, the spec-sheet stats and the sticker words come from the user's character; a martini and the white studio are only defaults, and a new set keeps the palette through the zine pages. Tone examples: \
```

### 1. Copy-Ready Prompt for Your Video Generator

```text
Subject & Product Replacement:
- Replace the original subject with (the character from the reference image) in every shot, maintaining strict facial identity, hair, and body proportions.
- Integrate (the product from the reference image) seamlessly into the scene, preserving exact logos, materials, colors, and physical reflections.
- Execute the sequence: STAY COOL — 20-second black-and-violet poster-motion edit starring the user.

INPUT
One clear photo of one person: the user, or someone who agreed to it. No public figures, no children. That person is the only star. Keep their face and identity exact...
- Camera & Motion: Replicate reference camera moves (dynamic pans, speed ramps, product close-ups, and whip transitions).
```

### 2. Original Example Prompt (Generated Video Shown)
```text
STAY COOL — 20-second black-and-violet poster-motion edit starring the user.

INPUT
One clear photo of one person: the user, or someone who agreed to it. No public figures, no children. That person is the only star. Keep their face and identity exactly the same in every shot. Never mix in a second person.

OUTPUT
- 20.000 s, 400 frames, 1440×1080 (4:3), 20 fps, H.264 + AAC.
- Keep the reference video's soundtrack exactly as it is. It is an original, license-free track made for this preset.
- Never use the audio of the generations. Never add or swap music.
- Cut everything on its 120 BPM grid: 1 beat = 10 frames, 1 bar = 40 frames, felt half-time.

LOOK
- Footage treatment: high-contrast halftone duotone in black → violet.
  - Shadows #0B0612. Mids #7B3BA9. Light lilac #D9B8EC only on faces and skin.
  - Fine 5 px halftone dots, crisp, strong contrast.
- Cut-outs: the person is cut out of every shot, with a thin 3 px lilac rim along the cut-out edge. The studio wall is never visible.
  - Every panel, window and card is a cut-out on a flat black (#0B0612) or violet (#7B3BA9) field.
  - Fields alternate black / violet. In the drop they flip on every cut.
  - A deep-violet (#3A1757) halftone-dot floor sits at the bottom of the frame.
- One orchid disc (#CB5AE0) runs through the whole edit:
  - it is born as a single dot;
  - it becomes the O of the words, a circle window onto the face, a halo behind the head, a hub, then a full flood.
  - Orchid is used ONLY for the disc.
- The edit ends on one pale flash frame (#F8ECFB). Hard cut. No titles, no end card, no logo.

TYPE
- Two faces only:
  - a condensed black grotesk in CAPS for the big words;
  - a high-contrast didone italic in lowercase for the small words.
- Text colour: paper #EBD6F1 or lilac #AD69CD.
- Words slam in on the beat and hold; letters can act as windows into the footage.
- The person may pass in front of the words, but every word must stay readable.
- Fixed copy, in this order, exactly: SLOW down · NO · NO RUSH · I SET THE pace · ON MY own time · every angle · NOTHING to prove · I DON'T chase · STILL me · EYES on me · KEEP up · YOUR MOVE · stay COOL.
- Never change, translate or add words. Never put the user's own text on screen.

FOOTAGE OF THE PERSON
Generate the person's set first:
- an identity HERO still from the photo;
- a full-body LOOK still with a locked outfit: plain white fitted top, open black jacket, black straight trousers, black boots, no logos;
- everything on a plain very light warm-grey seamless studio cyclorama, so the cut-outs come out clean.

Stills (Seedream 5 Pro):
- a washed full-body \
```

```text
Recreate the edit, not the footage. The product in this video is only an example: swap in the user's product and keep everything else.

Format: 4:3, ~32 s. About 105 cuts locked to a 125 BPM grid (beats, 8ths, 16ths, many 1–3 frame stabs).

Look: black studio void, single hard light sources only. Deep navy monochrome, crushed blacks, clipped whites, soft grain, vignette. One teal accent only for glows, thermal and x-ray; red only for the tracking HUD.

Shots: two thirds close-ups and macros of the product's textures, edges, lights and moving parts. Wides only as single beats: the product tiny at the end of a light seam, an overhead circle of light, a silhouette against a backlit wall.

Motif: a thin seam of white light on a glossy black floor. The product lands on it in the hook; in the finale the room light snaps off and only the product's own teal glow remains.

Effects, whole frame: crash zoom, bloom, white flash, line-art flip-book, thermal, x-ray, static, noise circle, datamosh, negative, posterize, slice, double exposure, tube strobe on the beat, micro-shake on impacts.

Ending: black, a small half-circle mark, black.

Rules: the product keeps its exact shape, colours and markings in every shot. No added text, logos or numbers. People are optional; never public figures. New original dark electronic score on the same grid with hits on the cuts; never reuse this video's track or licensed music.
```

## 🎬 Unboxing Virtual Try-On (Red Skeleton Print Hoodie)

* **ID:** `mkt-ugc_unboxing_virtual_try_on` | **Subcategory:** *UGC Try-On Ads*
* **Visible Effect:** Unboxing Virtual Try-On: a creator smashes a glass 'NEW DROP' box and tries on the red skeleton sherpa jacket.
* **Example Clip / Video Preview:** [Watch Video Clip (With Audio)](https://d8j0ntlcm91z4.cloudfront.net/marketing_studio_video_preset/367643d9-3fc5-43ae-aef5-0eeffd18d794-382ee9b9d34ccc3d.mp4)
* **Example Image Preview:** [View Image](https://d8j0ntlcm91z4.cloudfront.net/marketing_studio_preset_thumbnails/367643d9-3fc5-43ae-aef5-0eeffd18d794-851b8c239f2ed097.jpg)
* **Model Routing:** Keyframe: `Soul 2.0 (Character & Product Anchor)` | Video: `Seedance 2.5 (Commercial Direct-Response Engine)`
* **Format / Ratio:** `9:16` | **Duration:** `15s`

### 1. Copy-Ready Prompt for Your Video Generator
```text
Vertical 9:16 UGC video, natural handheld iPhone camera, soft natural daylight in (the bedroom or domestic setting).

Intro (0-3s): (The character from the reference image) holds up (the product from the reference image), speaking enthusiastically directly into the camera.

Cut 1 — Demonstration / Fit-Check (3-7s): Hard cut to (the character from the reference image) testing/wearing (the product from the reference image). They step back to show the full silhouette, doing a natural turn to display all angles and drape.

Cut 2 — Macro Detail (7-11s): Extreme close-up shot focusing sharply on the fabric texture, seams, and hardware of (the product from the reference image).

Cut 3 — Final Look (11-15s): Confident wrap shot of (the character from the reference image) smiling directly into the lens.

Audio & Performance: Authentic spoken dialogue with natural room tone: "(Spoken dialogue / review script for the product)". Lock exact facial features, skin tone, and hair of (the character from the reference image). Lock exact colors, logos, and silhouette of (the product from the reference image). Natural micro-expressions, no on-screen text, no distorted hands.
```

### 2. Original Example Prompt (Generated Video Shown)
```text
Unboxing Virtual Try-On: a creator smashes a glass 'NEW DROP' box and tries on the red skeleton sherpa jacket.
```

  Composite product and creator into starting frame matching the prompt brief: "Unboxing Virtual Try-On: a creator smashes a glass 'NEW DROP' box and tries on the red skeleton sherpa jacket.".

  Generate vertical video on Seedance 2.5 executing authentic creator action and seamless product focus.

* **ID:** `pixel-art` | **Subcategory:** *Retro Gaming & Pixel Styles*
* **Visible Effect:** Chunky pixelated sprites, dithered shading, colorful grid backgrounds, stepped retro animation
* **Example Clip / Video Preview:** [Watch Video Clip (With Audio)](https://cdn.higgsfield.ai/video_explainer_preset/d50f28ba-f206-49db-b7ea-dbb9e89fe2e0.mp4)

* **Model Routing:** Keyframe: `Flux.1 PixelArt / SDXL` | Video: `Seedance (12fps stepped I2V)`
* **Format / Ratio:** `16:9` | **Duration:** `10s–15s`

### 1. Copy-Ready Prompt for Your Video Generator
```text
An explainer video in retro 8-bit pixel-art style, chunky pixelated sprites of (the character from the reference image) and (the product/props from the reference image) on a (colorful grid background), crisp dithered shading, vibrant retro arcade palette, stepped 12fps 2D pixel animation of (the character/product performing the action).
```

### 2. Original Example Prompt (Generated Video Shown)
```text
An explainer video in retro 8-bit pixel-art style, chunky pixelated characters and props on a colorful grid background, crisp dithered shading and smooth pixel animation.
```

### 3. Step-by-Step Production Pipeline
* **Step 1 (Starting Keyframe):**
  Retro 8-bit pixel art illustration of subject on a vibrant grid background, chunky pixels, dithered shading, flat colors, clean arcade game aesthetic.
* **Step 2 (Video Motion Execution):**
  Chunky 8-bit pixel movement, subject moves with stepped retro arcade timing, camera locked 2D view, no 3D rotation, no smooth anti-aliased interpolation.
* **Negative Prompt (Guardrails):**
  `photorealistic, 3D CGI render, anti-aliased smooth curves, blurry pixels, modern soft gradients, realistic human anatomy`

* **Format / Ratio:** `9:16 (720x1280)` | **Duration:** `5s–10s`

### 1. Copy-Ready Prompt for Your Video Generator
```text
Commercial remake: Keep the exact camera choreography, speed curve, and timing of the reference video.

Subject & Product Replacement:
- Replace the original subject with (the character from the reference image) in every shot, maintaining strict facial identity, hair, and body proportions.
- Integrate (the product from the reference image) seamlessly into the scene, preserving exact logos, materials, colors, and physical reflections.
- Execute the sequence: Make a 20-second golden-hour sport aura edit, 16:9, for any sport the user names (default: rowing, double scull). Two athletes: the user's photos if given, otherwise a fictional young woman and young man in matching plain deep navy sport outfits. The...
- Camera & Motion: Replicate reference camera moves (dynamic pans, speed ramps, product close-ups, and whip transitions).
- Audio Sync: Keep background music, sound effects, and voice track synchronized with visual beats.
```

### 2. Original Example Prompt (Generated Video Shown)
```text
Make a 20-second golden-hour sport aura edit, 16:9, for any sport the user names (default: rowing, double scull). Two athletes: the user's photos if given, otherwise a fictional young woman and young man in matching plain deep navy sport outfits. Their gear is plain: no logos, lettering or numbers.

Look: shot straight into a low sun at sunrise or sunset. Strong backlight and lens flares, deep teal sky and shadows against warm gold highlights, halation. Vintage ultra-wide lens: barrel distortion, soft corners, chromatic fringing, vignette, film grain, gate weave. The sport's element (water spray, dust, snow, sand) flies through the backlight and glows gold.

Camera: moving in every shot. FPV dives and skims, orbits, crash zooms, fast tracking at ground or water level, handheld jolts. Slow motion on the impacts and on the aura moments.

Beats, in order:
1. Intro: athlete B slowly turns his head and stares into the lens, fast push-in, cut to black.
2. Drop: the gear slams into the element and blasts golden spray at the lens, white flash.
3. Fast cuts on the beat: hands on the grip, the drive, both athletes at full effort, an FPV dive over the place, a rush past, a high aerial.
4. A tape-stop: the action slows to a black-and-white freeze.
5. Break: athlete A glances into the lens with a slight confident smile, colour returns, then a flash montage.
6. Second drop: the two lift their gear as a silhouette against the sun.
7. An eighth-note run with black-and-white step-print inserts.
8. The two bump fists: freeze and flash on the touch.
9. They move away into the sun, the light blooms, fade to black.

Edit: cut on a 125 BPM grid. Speed ramps with motion blur, zoom punches, camera jolts, RGB split, whip, spin and zoom-through transitions. Music: an original dark phonk-style track (cowbell melody, distorted 808, hard claps) with its hits on the cuts.

No text, captions, logos or watermark. No real people unless the user supplies them. Nobody else in the frame.
```

### 3. Step-by-Step Production Pipeline
* **Step 1 (Starting Keyframe):**
  Frame 0 match: Composite user product/character reference onto starting frame of https://static-public-media.higgsfield.ai/katana-presets/6c3ad48f-9a0d-4498-85b8-1aa5e13517a5.mp4. Match focal length and lighting.

```text
Look: black-and-white high-contrast film with heavy grain; only red objects stay in colour (headphones, lipstick, nails, laser, spray paint). Cuts on a heartbeat accelerating from 80 to 215 BPM with a light hit and camera kick on every beat, white flash on the drop, red spray filling the lens at 16.3 s, final hero close-up with slow push-in from 16.8 s to 20 s. Keep this video's heartbeat score.
Never speed up footage (real time, the final shot slightly slowed). No text, titles, HUD or watermark. No repeated shots, no black gaps. Photoreal faces only; no public figures, no one under 18.
```
